People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

It sounds real clear, like someone’s right beside me, but when I look around, it’s quiet and nobody else reacts.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you're hearing telling you to do something or are they more critical of what's going on with the patient records?\n\nPatient: They’re mostly saying which records were changed. Not really… telling me to do anything, just pointing it out, like, “that chart’s been altered.” It comes through the speaker or the vents, clear as someone right next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to take any specific action regarding the patient records they mention?\n\nPatient: No… not really specific actions. They just say which records were changed, like they’re pointing it out through the speaker or the vents. It sounds right there, but nobody else seems to hear it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to respond or react in some way when the voices point out the altered patient records?\n\nPatient: I… I feel like I should pay attention, at least. When it comes from the call-light speaker or the vents that clearly, like somebody’s right next to me saying a record was changed, it’s hard not to react… even if nobody else hears it.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these voices pointing out the altered patient records?\n\nPatient: Uneasy. Like… on edge, I guess. It sounds so clear, like it’s coming right out of the call-light speaker or the vents, somebody standing there saying which chart was changed. And when nobody else hears it, that makes me feel… I don’t know. Worried.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep track of what the voices are saying or to make sense of their messages?\n\nPatient: Not usually. It’s… pretty clear when it happens. Like it’s coming from the call-light speaker or the hallway vents, saying which records were changed. I don’t really have to piece it together. It just… says it.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices affected your ability to work or interact with your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I was taken off the floor during a shift because my supervisor said my handoffs and charting weren’t safe anymore.\n\nI don’t really like talking about it. My husband’s been handling things at home I normally do, too. It feels like… my reputation at work, and LPN school, all of that is kind of falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy, energetic, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. There are days I feel like I’m finally awake, like I’m supposed to fix things at work. But if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I get really irritated. I snap at them… like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

{"intent": "Determine the presence of manic episodes", "question": "Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or energetic that lasted for several days or longer?"}

When it comes from the call-light speaker or the vents that clearly, like somebody’s right next to me saying a record was changed, it’s hard not to react… even if nobody else hears it.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these voices pointing out the altered patient records?\n\nPatient: Uneasy. Like… on edge, I guess. It sounds so clear, like it’s coming right out of the call-light speaker or the vents, somebody standing there saying which chart was changed. And when nobody else hears it, that makes me feel… I don’t know. Worried.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep track of what the voices are saying or to make sense of their messages?\n\nPatient: Not usually. It’s… pretty clear when it happens. Like it’s coming from the call-light speaker or the hallway vents, saying which records were changed. I don’t really have to piece it together. It just… says it.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices affected your ability to work or interact with your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I was taken off the floor during a shift because my supervisor said my handoffs and charting weren’t safe anymore.\n\nI don’t really like talking about it. My husband’s been handling things at home I normally do, too. It feels like… my reputation at work, and LPN school, all of that is kind of falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy, energetic, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. There are days I feel like I’m finally awake, like I’m supposed to fix things at work. But if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I get really irritated. I snap at them… like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or energetic that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yes… for a few days sometimes. I’ll feel like I’m finally awake, like I’m supposed to fix everything at work. But then if my husband or one of the nurses tells me to slow down, I get… really irritated. Like they’re blocking something important.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like you're finally awake and supposed to fix things at work, do you find yourself being more productive or taking on more tasks than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… I’ve been hearing things at work. Voices, I guess. From the call-light speaker, sometimes the hallway vents. They’re telling me patient records have been changed. It sounds clear, like someone is standing right there, but the unit is quiet and no one else seems to hear it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what the voices are saying and how it relates to your work with patient records?\n\nPatient: They… they’ll say certain charts aren’t right. Like a record was changed, or something was switched. It comes through the call-light speaker, sometimes the vents in the hall. It sounds real clear, like someone’s right beside me, but when I look around, it’s quiet and nobody else reacts.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you're hearing telling you to do something or are they more critical of what's going on with the patient records?\n\nPatient: They’re mostly saying which records were changed. Not really… telling me to do anything, just pointing it out, like, “that chart’s been altered.” It comes through the speaker or the vents, clear as someone right next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to take any specific action regarding the patient records they mention?\n\nPatient: No… not really specific actions. They just say which records were changed, like they’re pointing it out through the speaker or the vents. It sounds right there, but nobody else seems to hear it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to respond or react in some way when the voices point out the altered patient records?\n\nPatient: I… I feel like I should pay attention, at least. When it comes from the call-light speaker or the vents that clearly, like somebody’s right next to me saying a record was changed, it’s hard not to react… even if nobody else hears it.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these voices pointing out the altered patient records?\n\nPatient: Uneasy. Like… on edge, I guess. It sounds so clear, like it’s coming right out of the call-light speaker or the vents, somebody standing there saying which chart was changed. And when nobody else hears it, that makes me feel… I don’t know. Worried.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep track of what the voices are saying or to make sense of their messages?\n\nPatient: Not usually. It’s… pretty clear when it happens. Like it’s coming from the call-light speaker or the hallway vents, saying which records were changed. I don’t really have to piece it together. It just… says it.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices affected your ability to work or interact with your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I was taken off the floor during a shift because my supervisor said my handoffs and charting weren’t safe anymore.\n\nI don’t really like talking about it. Like they’re blocking something important.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like you're finally awake and supposed to fix things at work, do you find yourself being more productive or taking on more tasks than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. It’s been… about two months now, since they asked me to help lead that wound-care documentation rollout.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling really awake and energetic, do you find you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure. I mean, my sleep’s been off, but I don’t know if I’d say I need less. Like a record was changed, or something was switched. It comes through the call-light speaker, sometimes the vents in the hall. It sounds real clear, like someone’s right beside me, but when I look around, it’s quiet and nobody else reacts.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you're hearing telling you to do something or are they more critical of what's going on with the patient records?\n\nPatient: They’re mostly saying which records were changed. Not really… telling me to do anything, just pointing it out, like, “that chart’s been altered.” It comes through the speaker or the vents, clear as someone right next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to take any specific action regarding the patient records they mention?\n\nPatient: No… not really specific actions. Like… on edge, I guess. It sounds so clear, like it’s coming right out of the call-light speaker or the vents, somebody standing there saying which chart was changed. It sounds real clear, like someone’s right beside me, but when I look around, it’s quiet and nobody else reacts.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you're hearing telling you to do something or are they more critical of what's going on with the patient records?\n\nPatient: They’re mostly saying which records were changed. Not really… telling me to do anything, just pointing it out, like, “that chart’s been altered.” It comes through the speaker or the vents, clear as someone right next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to take any specific action regarding the patient records they mention?\n\nPatient: No… not really specific actions. They just say which records were changed, like they’re pointing it out through the speaker or the vents. It sounds right there, but nobody else seems to hear it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to respond or react in some way when the voices point out the altered patient records?\n\nPatient: I… I feel like I should pay attention, at least. When it comes from the call-light speaker or the vents that clearly, like somebody’s right next to me saying a record was changed, it’s hard not to react… even if nobody else hears it.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these voices pointing out the altered patient records?\n\nPatient: Uneasy. Like… on edge, I guess. It sounds so clear, like it’s coming right out of the call-light speaker or the vents, somebody standing there saying which chart was changed. And when nobody else hears it, that makes me feel… I don’t know. Worried.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep track of what the voices are saying or to make sense of their messages?\n\nPatient: Not usually. It’s… pretty clear when it happens. Like it’s coming from the call-light speaker or the hallway vents, saying which records were changed. I don’t really have to piece it together. It just… says it.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices affected your ability to work or interact with your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I was taken off the floor during a shift because my supervisor said my handoffs and charting weren’t safe anymore.\n\nI don’t really like talking about it. My husband’s been handling things at home I normally do, too. It feels like… my reputation at work, and LPN school, all of that is kind of falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy, energetic, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. There are days I feel like I’m finally awake, like I’m supposed to fix things at work. But if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I get really irritated. I snap at them… like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt abnormally happy or energetic that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yes… for a few days sometimes. I’ll feel like I’m finally awake, like I’m supposed to fix everything at work. But then if my husband or one of the nurses tells me to slow down, I get… really irritated. Like they’re blocking something important.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like you're finally awake and supposed to fix things at work, do you find yourself being more productive or taking on more tasks than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. It’s been… about two months now, since they asked me to help lead that wound-care documentation rollout.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling really awake and energetic, do you find you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure. I mean, my sleep’s been off, but I don’t know if I’d say I need less. I just don’t always notice it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices you're hearing are actually telling you the truth about the patient records being changed?\n\nPatient: I… yes. I think they’re telling the truth.\n\nThe wound-care charts don’t look right. There’s a pattern there, and management doesn’t want it seen. My supervisor says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, but… I can tell they’re watching what I write now. Because I’m the one who caught it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you're hearing could be mistaken about the patient records being changed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.\n\nI know what I’m seeing in those wound-care charts. It’s a pattern, not just late entries or coding errors. My supervisor can say that, but… I can tell they’re watching what I write now, because I’m the one who noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the changes in the patient records you're seeing are just mistakes or misinterpretations, rather than an actual conspiracy?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s just mistakes.\n\nI know what I saw in the wound-care charts. There’s a pattern there, and management doesn’t want it seen. My supervisor keeps saying it’s late entries or coding errors, but… I can tell they’re watching what I write now. I’m the one who caught it.\n\nDoctor: If someone could prove to you that the patient records were not actually changed, would you be able to accept that?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… if it was real proof, maybe I’d have to look at it.\n\nBut right now I know what I saw in those wound-care charts. It’s not just coding mistakes. There’s a pattern, and… they don’t want it noticed. I can tell they’re watching what I write because I caught it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
